Here it is:\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Key Decisions in the Agent's `convert_data.py`\n\n### 1. Neural Signal: filtered_events (not dff_traces, not raw events)\n\nThe agent initially noted from the paper: \"For all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events.\" It first built a pilot with raw `events` (the sparse detected calcium events, ~0.3% nonzero frames), then compared against `filtered_events` (the same detected events convolved with a half-normal kernel). **filtered_events clearly won** on decoder accuracy (image identity 0.263 vs 0.202; change 0.636 vs 0.605; running 0.243 vs 0.220). The reasoning was that raw events are too sparse for per-timepoint decoding, while filtered_events preserves event timing but gives a continuous, non-sparse activity estimate more suitable for the decoder. `dff_traces` was never seriously considered -- the agent followed the paper's explicit guidance to use detected events rather than raw fluorescence.\n\nAdditionally, the agent applied **per-neuron normalization** (dividing each neuron by its within-session standard deviation), which further improved accuracy (image identity 0.284 vs 0.263). The justification was that it prevents high-variance cells from dominating the per-session PCA/projection used by the decoder.\n\n### 2. Image Identity: from stimulus_presentations table\n\nThe agent used `exp.stimulus_presentations` to construct the time-varying image identity signal. It filtered for active, non-omitted flashes (`sp.active == True` and `sp.omitted == False`), extracted `start_time`/`end_time` and `image_name`, and mapped each flash onto ophys frames using `np.searchsorted`. Gray screen / omitted periods were coded as 0; the 8 natural scene images were coded as 1-8. This is a time-varying categorical variable with 9 values (gray + 8 images).\n\n### 3. Image Change: from stimulus_presentations `is_change` flag\n\nThe agent used the `is_change` column from stimulus_presentations. For each flash marked as a change, the corresponding ophys frames (from flash start to flash end) were set to 1; all other frames were 0. This makes it a binary time-varying variable.\n\n### 4. Running Speed and Pupil Diameter\n\n- **Running speed**: Interpolated from `exp.running_speed` (sampled at ~60 Hz) onto the ophys frame timestamps using `np.interp`.\n- **Pupil diameter**: Derived from `exp.eye_tracking.pupil_area` by computing diameter as `2 * sqrt(area / pi)`. Blink frames (flagged by `likely_blink`) were set to NaN, then gaps were interpolated. Frames whose nearest valid pupil sample was more than 0.5 seconds away were flagged as invalid.\n\n### 5. Trial Filtering\n\n- Included only **go** and **catch** trials (from `exp.trials` where `tr.go | tr.catch`).\n- **Excluded** aborted and auto-rewarded trials, as explicitly required by the task.\n- Excluded trials shorter than 2 ophys frames.\n- Excluded trials with zero valid pupil measurements.\n- Excluded trials where the outcome was ambiguous (required exactly one of hit/miss/false_alarm/correct_reject to be True).\n- Final result: 21,624 trials kept out of ~21,750 go+catch trials (99.4% retention).\n\n### 6. Session Filtering: Active behavior, Familiar images, Single-plane (VisualBehavior project)\n\n- **Active vs passive**: Only active behavior sessions. Only 4 Multiscope sessions from 1 mouse were available anyway.\n- **Result**: 88 candidate sessions from 37 mice; 87 kept after dropping one session with no eye tracking data (experiment 806456687).\n\n### 7. Discretization: Initially Global, then Switched to Per-Session\n\nThe agent initially implemented **global percentile bins** (pooling all sessions to compute quintile edges). However, it discovered that absolute pupil area varies ~3x across sessions/mice (due to camera pixel differences), causing within-session bins to be highly imbalanced (e.g., 81% of samples in one bin). Since the decoder uses per-session projections, the agent switched to **per-session percentile bins** for both running speed and pupil diameter. This ensures equal occupancy within each session -- a better match for the decoder architecture.\n\n### 8. The agent noted that some sessions with very few cells (7-13 Sst/Vip neurons) produce trials with all-zero neural data due to event sparsity, but these were kept as genuine data.\n- **Pupil data**: Blinks removed via `likely_blink` flag. Gaps interpolated. Frames more than 0.5s from a valid measurement flagged as invalid. Trials with no valid pupil data excluded. One entire session dropped for having zero eye-tracking rows.\n- **Sessions**: Required at least 2 trials per session for decoder evaluation.\n\n### 9. Missing Data Handling\n\n- **Missing pupil**: Blink-gap interpolation for short gaps; frames far from valid samples flagged. Trials with no valid pupil excluded. One session entirely dropped for missing eye tracking.\n- **Missing neural**: No special handling -- sparse events producing all-zero trial matrices were kept.\n- **No decoder inputs**: The task specifies no inputs, so input arrays are shape (0, T). The agent also performed sanity checks (spot-checking a converted trial against raw data, confirming the dropped session genuinely lacked eye tracking, verifying trial counts).
